#### I60 · Flag lifecycle is maintained imperatively in many places: term_validation and synthesis_verify flags stack or are deleted instead of resolved, sync_awaiting_flags is called from seven sites, and rescreen_rule_only resolves flags itself first

*confirmed · medium · extraction-rob-synthesis · reasons: R2*

Flag lifecycle is imperative and inconsistent: extract_one (sragent/stages/extraction.py:332-339) adds term_validation flags without resolving open ones (store.upsert keeps flags across versions), revalidate_terms (396-403) deletes open and resolved term_validation flags from n['flags'] instead of resolving them, and actions.edit kind=ext (actions.py:148-153) re-validates but neither flags nor resolves. verify_one (synthesis.py:152-157) adds synthesis_verify flags without resolving open ones; repair_one resolves them first (178-180) but a statement left faulted after repair (194) and later revised by the engine (apply_revised pops only 'verification', feedback.py:186; engine.py:433-436 re-verifies) gets a second open flag; actions.edit syn resolves them with its own loop (216-218). sync_awaiting_flags is called from seven sites (screening.py:425, 508, 583; engine.py:349; sync.py:50; feedback.py:406; extraction.py:285) and rescreen_rule_only (screening.py:571-573) resolves the same flags itself before calling it (583). todos.apply (todos.py:446-448) resolves every open flag on a screen node, whereas actions.edit screen (129-131) filters by source. Because the To-do builder (todos.py:194) lists each open flag, duplicates appear as duplicate cards and counts.

Scenario: term_validation: extract_one (extraction.py:332-339) flags every not_found term on the ext node after upsert and never looks at existing open term_validation flags; store.upsert (store.py:76-108) keeps the node's 'flags' list across versions, so a second extraction of the same study (engine._extract on a stale/new-field node, actions.rerun, process_pdfs) appends a second open flag for the same term. revalidate_terms (396-403) does n['flags'] = [f for f in n['flags'] if source != 'term_validation'] (398), deleting open and resolved ones (history lost) and then re-flagging. actions.edit kind=ext (148-153) recomputes item['terms'] but neither flags a new not_found nor resolves the open term_validation flag the person just fixed. synthesis_verify: verify_one (synthesis.py:152-157) flags without resolving earlier open ones; repair_one resolves them first (178-180) and verify_one again, but when a statement stays faulted after repair (194, 'it stays, with the check's flag open') and is later touched by the engine — _revise_dependent -> apply_revised (feedback.py:181-191) pops 'verification' (186) and leaves flags — engine._synthesis verify_one (engine.py:433-436) adds a second open synthesis_verify flag; actions.edit syn resolves them (216-218) by its own loop. sync_awaiting_flags: grep confirms 7 call sites (screening.py:425, 508, 583; engine.py:349; sync.py:50; feedback.py:406; extraction.py:285). rescreen_rule_only resolves SCREEN_FLAG_SOURCES flags itself (screening.py:571-573) before calling sync_awaiting_flags (583), which would resolve them anyway (543-545). todos.apply screen branch (todos.py:446-448) resolves every open flag on the screen node regardless of source, while actions.edit kind=screen (actions.py:129-131) resolves only sources screening/None/feedback and skips 'pending review' texts. The To-do builder (todos.py:194) lists every open flag of those sources, so duplicates become duplicate cards.

Evidence:
- `sragent/stages/extraction.py:332` — extract_one: flag not_found terms (336-339) without resolving open term_validation flags; upsert keeps flags across versions (store.py:76-108)
- `sragent/stages/extraction.py:398` — revalidate_terms replaces n['flags'] (open and resolved term_validation flags removed) then re-flags 399-403
- `sragent/actions.py:148` — edit ext: re-validates terms into item['terms'], no flag/resolve
- `sragent/stages/synthesis.py:152` — verify_one flags synthesis_verify without resolving earlier open ones; repair_one resolves first (178-180)
- `sragent/feedback.py:186` — apply_revised pops 'verification' only; flags untouched
- `sragent/engine.py:433` — _synthesis: verify_one on touched statements after revision -> second open flag if still faulted
- `sragent/stages/screening.py:571` — rescreen_rule_only resolves SCREEN_FLAG_SOURCES flags (571-573) then sync_awaiting_flags (583), which resolves them itself (543-545)
- `sragent/todos.py:446` — apply: resolves every open flag on the screen node regardless of source; actions.edit screen (129-131) filters by source

#### I84 · Study status is derived in six modules with their own rules and strings (included_ids, studies.status_of, steps.py, report.prisma and _flow, todos conditions, engine.plan, needed_pdfs); the Studies 'status' is a free-text vocabulary of 15+ strings used as filter and export column; two PRISMA implementations disagree

*confirmed · medium · human-loop · reasons: R1, R4*

Study status is derived in several places with their own rules and strings. store.included_ids (sragent/store.py:276-282) defines inclusion as any active screen node with decision=='include' (no stage check) and is reused by todos (rob, 261-266), engine.plan (engine.py:62) and both PRISMA functions, so inclusion membership itself is centralised. What is duplicated: studies.status_of (studies.py:43-84) composes 16+ hand-written labels from decision/decided_by/rule/stage (and its include+title_abstract branch at line 81 is unreachable behind the include return at 63); ui/steps.py:13-47 recounts the same fields with different wording ('waiting for full-text review', 'not retrieved (PDF requested)', 'unreadable'); todos.py:113, engine.plan (engine.py:85) and fulltext.needed_pdfs (fulltext.py:219-242) each test decision/stage for the pending sub-states. The Studies tab filter is built from the distinct label strings (index.html:1427, exact match at 1416), chipFor colours by prefix (499), the CSV export writes the string (server.py:1004-1008 via studies.to_csv), and the Overview KPIs use the three groups (678-686). report.prisma (report.py:19-47) and _flow (497-529) define 'sought' (27-28 vs 518) and rule exclusions (34 vs 517) differently; report.md uses prisma (64) and report_evidence.html uses _flow (621), so when a rule is applied to an already-screened record (reachable via engine.py:242, ui/server.py:115, snowball.py:52) the two files of the same run print different 'sought' and 'excluded by rules' numbers; cli status (cli.py:145) and the stored report node (report.py:930) use prisma().

Scenario: PRISMA disagreement, traced: (1) A record screened at title/abstract gets decision 'forward' (stage=title_abstract). Later screening rules are re-applied to already-screened records: reachable from engine.py:242 (update engine after a protocol change), sragent/ui/server.py:115 (UI apply-rules endpoint), snowball.py:52 (date-range exclude on citation-searched records) and snowball.py:130, screening.py:510/575. rules.apply (sragent/rules.py:97-101) skips only human-decided nodes, copies the existing data (keeps stage) and sets decision=exclude, rule=X, decision_before_rule='forward' (rules.exclude at 132-141 does the same and even retires ext/rob nodes, i.e. it is designed for already-screened records). (2) write_reports (report.py:926-930) writes report.md via report_markdown, which uses prisma() (report.py:64, printed at 76-83), and report_evidence.html via report_html, which uses _flow() (report.py:621, figure at 752 built by _prisma_figure 532-586). (3) prisma (report.py:27-28): sought = stage in (retrieval, full_text) OR (rule and decision_before_rule not in (None,'exclude')) -> counts the forward-then-ruled record. _flow (report.py:518): sought = len(ret)+len(ft) -> does not. report.md prints 'full texts sought: K+1'; the HTML figure prints 'Reports sought for retrieval (n = K)'. (4) Rule exclusions: prisma excluded_by_rules (report.py:34) counts rule+exclude at any stage; _flow excluded_screen_rules (report.py:517) counts only title_abstract-stage rule exclusions. A full-text-stage record (decision include/awaiting) that is later rule-excluded keeps stage=full_text, so report.md's 'excluded by publication-type rules' includes it while the HTML 'Records excluded / Publication-type rules (n=...)' box does not (it surfaces instead under 'Reports excluded' reasons at full text). cli status (cli.py:142-145) prints prisma(); the report node stores prisma() (report.py:930); the HTML shows _flow(). Status vocabulary, traced: store.included_ids (store.py:276-282) = active and decision=='include', no stage check. studies.status_of (studies.py:43-84) returns 16+ hand-written labels ('not screened yet', 'excluded (rule)', 'excluded (you)', 'excluded (extraction + verifier)', 'excluded (verifier)', 'excluded (agent, <stage>)', 'included (you)', 'included (verifier)', 'included', 'undecided: screener and verifier disagree', 'undecided: agents disagree after extraction', 'undecided after full-text review', 'full text not retrieved[ (declared not obtainable)]', 'full text unreadable', 'forwarded to full-text review', '<decision> (<stage>)'); its branch at line 81 for decision=='include' and stage=='title_abstract' is unreachable because the include branch at line 63 returns first. ui/steps.py:13-47 recounts the same decision/stage fields with different wording ('included after full-text review', 'undecided', 'waiting for full-text review', 'not retrieved (PDF requested)', 'unreadable'). studies_list (studies.py:100-120) puts the label in row['status']; server.py:1004-1008 exports those rows through studies.to_csv (studies.py:341-349), which writes every key including 'status'. index.html:1427 builds the status <select> from the distinct strings and filteredStudies (1416) matches r.status exactly; chipFor (499) colours by startsWith('included'/'excluded'); the Overview KPIs (678-686) use r.group. todos.py:113 tests decision=='awaiting' and no human_decision for eligibility; fulltext.needed_pdfs (fulltext.py:219-242) tests fulltext_unavailable / parse_failed / awaiting+pdf_wanted; engine.plan (engine.py:57-85) tests rule and stage in (title_abstract, retrieval). Over-claim found: todos.py:261-266 (rob) and engine.plan:62 reuse store.included_ids rather than their own include rule, so inclusion membership is centralised; the duplication is in pending/forward/not-retrieved sub-states, label strings, and the two PRISMA definitions.

Evidence:
- `sragent/stages/report.py:27` — prisma(): sought includes rule-excluded nodes whose decision_before_rule is not None/exclude; excluded_by_rules (34) counts any stage
- `sragent/stages/report.py:517` — _flow(): excluded_screen_rules counts title_abstract stage only; sought (518) = len(ret)+len(ft) with no rule term
- `sragent/stages/report.py:64` — report_markdown uses prisma(); printed at 76-83 into report.md
- `sragent/stages/report.py:621` — report_html uses _flow(); figure at 752 via _prisma_figure (532-586)

#### I85 · The screening decision rule is encoded in four places (prompt, settle/rule_decision, feedback TYPE_HINTS, GUIDE) and the To-do suggestion logic re-implements it instead of calling screening.decide/rederive; the citation guard is re-implemented in four places

*confirmed · medium · screening · reasons: R5, R4*

The screening decision rule is encoded in four places: the SCREEN prompt asks the model for a decision that the code keeps only as llm_decision (prompts.py:142; screening.py:221) while settle + rule_decision derive the stored decision (screening.py:70-99, 142-175); feedback.TYPE_HINTS repeats the rule in prose (feedback.py:43-46) and GUIDE.md:454 again. todos.build re-implements it for the eligibility suggestion (_bad/not_met at todos.py:118-127, read_full and pending_disagreement branches at 129-150) instead of calling decide/rederive or needs_fulltext_review, and the copy omits the any_of group rule, so a record kept 'awaiting' because an any_of group is met can be pre-filled with 'exclude: <member> fails'; studies.status_of and sync_awaiting_flags render the same states with their own strings. The synthesis citation guard, by contrast, has a single implementation (synthesis.reconcile_citations) called from write_statements, repair_one, feedback.apply_revised and actions.edit; what differs is the handling of its result (apply_revised discards the fixes and does not resolve flags, actions.edit stores citation_fixes and resolves synthesis_verify flags), plus a redundant in-line cite drop in engine.py:406-410 before apply_revised reconciles again.

Scenario: Decision rule: prompts.SCREEN asks for a holistic 'decision' (prompts.py:142); screen_one keeps it only as llm_decision (screening.py:221) and derives the stored decision from settle (70-99) + rule_decision (142-175) via decide (118-124). feedback.TYPE_HINTS['screen'] restates the rule in prose (feedback.py:43-46) and GUIDE.md row 9.3 (line 454) again. todos.build re-implements it for the eligibility suggestion: _bad (todos.py:118-123) classifies a criterion as failing/unclear from its type and assessment, not_met (126-127) picks the failing ones, and read_full / pending_disagreement branches (129-150) build the suggestion; it never calls decide/rederive/needs_fulltext_review. Concrete divergence: rule_decision treats inclusion criteria sharing an any_of label as a group that is met when any member is met (screening.py:155-157, 165-171), while _bad/not_met ignore any_of. A full-text record with I1a (any_of 'A') not_met, I1b (any_of 'A') met and another criterion unclear is stored as 'awaiting' (group met, one unclear), yet the To-do card suggests {'option':'exclude','criterion':'I1a','why':'agent: I1a fails'}, contradicting the rule that decided the record. studies.status_of (studies.py:54-83) and sync_awaiting_flags (screening.py:521-537) render the same states with their own strings. Citation guard, partly refuted: there is one implementation, synthesis.reconcile_citations (synthesis.py:23-40), and all four cited sites call it: write_statements (synthesis.py:70), repair_one (186), feedback.apply_revised (feedback.py:183) and actions.edit syn (actions.py:208). They differ only in what they do with its output: write_statements, repair_one and actions.edit store citation_fixes on the node, apply_revised discards the fixes ('cites, _ ='); actions.edit additionally resolves open synthesis_verify flags (actions.py:216-218) while apply_revised does not. engine.py:406-410 drops a no-longer-included cite in-line and then calls feedback.apply_revised, which runs reconcile_citations anyway, so the in-line drop is a redundant partial duplicate rather than a fourth implementation.

Evidence:
- `sragent/prompts.py:142` — model schema 'decision': include|exclude|uncertain
- `sragent/stages/screening.py:221` — 'llm_decision': out.get('decision'); stored decision from decide() at 215
- `sragent/stages/screening.py:142` — rule_decision incl. any_of group handling (155-157, 165-171); settle at 70
- `sragent/feedback.py:43` — TYPE_HINTS['screen'] restates the rule in prose
- `sragent/todos.py:118` — _bad / not_met (126-127) re-implement the rule without any_of; suggestion built at 129-150 without decide/rederive
- `sragent/studies.py:70` — status_of renders awaiting states with its own strings; sync_awaiting_flags (screening.py:521-537) likewise
- `sragent/stages/synthesis.py:23` — reconcile_citations: the single citation-guard implementation
- `sragent/stages/synthesis.py:70` — write_statements calls reconcile_citations; repair_one at 186

#### I102 · Two HTTP layers with different identity, caching and rate limiting; OpenAlex is configured only inside snowball so the fulltext stage calls it anonymously; `sragent data` hard-codes '.cache' and skips pubmed.configure

*not yet verified · low · tools · reasons: R5*

sources/http.get uses UA 'sragent/0.1' with a disk cache and per-host spacing (sragent/sources/http.py:21, 33-45); fulltext._download_oa and keep_fetched_files call requests.get directly with UA 'sragent/0.2 (...)' (fulltext.py:73-74, 110-111) and docparse.azure_analyze likewise (docparse.py:49-57), so concurrent fetch_one calls have no shared backoff and re-running a stage re-downloads PDFs. openalex.MAILTO is module-global and filled only by openalex.configure, whose sole caller is snowball.py:33; fetch_one calls openalex.work at fulltext.py:190 with whatever MAILTO holds, contradicting configs/README.md:133. cmd_data calls http.init_cache('.cache') and never pubmed.configure (cli.py:280-283) while Ctx.create uses cfg.cache_dir and configures email/api_key (context.py:54-57); cache keys include email/api_key (http.py:33) so data fetched via `sragent data` is not reused by runs with a key.

Evidence:
- `sragent/stages/fulltext.py:73` — raw requests.get with its own UA; keep_fetched_files 110-111
- `sragent/sources/http.py:33` — cached, rate-limited get with UA 'sragent/0.1'
- `sragent/stages/snowball.py:33` — openalex.configure(...) — the only caller; fulltext.py:190 calls openalex.work
- `sragent/cli.py:280` — cmd_data: http.init_cache('.cache'); no pubmed.configure

*not yet verified · low · tools · reasons: R5*

search.dedup matches PMID, DOI and near-identical titles (sragent/stages/search.py:30-55) and is called only at 194; snowball matches by pmid/doi/openalex and rec: existence (snowball.py:43-44, 86, 97, 118) and feedback._add_paper checks rec:<pmid> only (feedback.py:627), so a preprint/journal pair found by chasing enters twice while GUIDE.md:208 promises de-duplication. synergy.load returns the existing data/<key>_records.jsonl (synergy.py:31-32) and `sragent data` writes the same path (cli.py:283). pipeline._fulltext -> screening.fulltext_review (pipeline.py:37; screening.py:500) while stages/fulltext.py exports fetch_one/needed_pdfs only and screening.py:14's docstring omits the NCBI/Unpaywall/OpenAlex fallbacks. search.search runs build_query before the kind=='file' branch (search.py:164-168), so a model call and a recorded strategy that never executed are printed in the report (report.py:708-710).

#### I82 · Three different orderings of the to-do list: backend sort by (STEPS, priority, id), the UI regroups by kind, next_action uses a third priority chain; the backend 'priority' field is dead data

*confirmed · low · human-loop · reasons: R7*

todos.build sorts by (STEPS order, priority, id) (sragent/todos.py:354-355); renderTodo regroups by kind in its own fixed order (sragent/ui/static/index.html:731-758: protocol, fields, search, feedback, eligibility, sync, fulltexts, edits, flags, rob, ruled), so the backend's cross-kind order never reaches the screen; next_action (todos.py:631-691) uses a third ladder (feedback, eligibility, pdfs, sync, run, edits, flags, rob, fulltexts). 'priority' is dead data: its only use is the sort, and it is constant within every kind, so it never changes any visible order; 'step' is still used by ui/steps.py:13. The Next-step banner can point to a section below another open item (open flag + open fulltexts after synthesis -> #g-flags, which sits under #g-fulltexts), but the ladder does this deliberately (fulltexts is last 'Optional' after synthesis). GUIDE.md:280-293 documents the UI order only and itself lists flags before edits, opposite to the UI.

Scenario: (1) todos.build (sragent/todos.py:47) appends every to-do with a 'step' and a 'priority' and sorts at line 355 by (STEPS index, priority, id). Resulting backend order: protocol[plan] -> search -> eligibility[screen] -> fulltexts[fulltext] -> extract{manual_edit p2, flag p3, fields p9} -> rob{flag p3, rob p4} -> synthesis{sync p1, flag p3} -> feedback{changeset/clarify/disagreement p0, manual_edit p2, flag p3}. (2) The server hands that list to the UI as S.todos; renderTodo (sragent/ui/static/index.html:720) filters by kind with k(kind) and pushes groups in its own fixed order at lines 737-758: protocol, fields, search, feedback, eligibility, sync, fulltexts, edits, flags, rob (required), rob (optional), ruled. The cross-kind backend order therefore never reaches the screen. Within one kind every item carries the same constant priority (eligibility 1, fulltexts 2, flag 3, manual_edit 2, rob 4, changeset/clarify/disagreement 0, sync 1, fields 9), so 'priority' changes no visible order either. grep shows no other consumer of t['priority'] in Python or JS (index.html:1048 'x.priority===1' is fulltext.needed_pdfs' separate per-PDF field). 'step' IS consumed by ui/steps.py:13 (tcount per step), so only 'priority' is dead. (3) next_action (todos.py:631-691) has a third ladder: feedback -> eligibility -> new_files(pdfs) -> sync -> waiting_for_pdfs -> no synthesis -> run -> manual_edit -> flag -> rob -> fulltexts(pdfs page). Concrete divergence: synthesis written, one open flag, and a 'fulltexts' to-do (built at todos.py:170-187 with no 'optional' key, so it is in open_t at line 634). next_action returns go=todo group=flags (line 684); goNext/goTodoGroup (index.html:657-663) scrolls to #g-flags (line 756), which is rendered below #g-fulltexts (line 754). The banner points below an open item; but line 689 shows the ladder deliberately ranks fulltexts last after synthesis ('Optional: add full texts...'), so this is a design choice, not a defect. (4) GUIDE.md:280-293 documents the page order only, and is itself slightly off: it lists 'Points the agent flagged' (291) before 'Your edits' (292) while renderTodo renders edits (755) before flags (756).

Evidence:
- `sragent/todos.py:355` — todos.sort(key=lambda t: (order.get(t['step'], 99), t['priority'], t['id'])) - only use of 'priority'
- `sragent/todos.py:631` — def next_action: separate priority ladder (kind checks at 665-691); fulltexts last at 689
- `sragent/ui/static/index.html:731` — renderTodo: 'groups' jump list in fixed kind order; sections pushed in that order at 737-758
- `sragent/ui/static/index.html:754` — #g-fulltexts rendered above #g-edits (755), #g-flags (756), #g-rob
- `sragent/ui/steps.py:13` — tcount = Counter(t['step'] ...): 'step' is consumed; 'priority' is not consumed anywhere
- `GUIDE.md:280` — 'What the To-do page contains, top to bottom' table (280-293) documents the UI order; lists flags (291) before edits (292), opposite of the UI

### State that goes out of sync (14)

#### I56 · Proposals (feedback/*.json) are invisible to the engine: one manual edit creates two competing to-dos (proposal via propagate_from vs applied via 'Update now'), 'Update now' redoes a proposal's work on the live store, and proposals are only invalidated at accept

*not yet verified · high · graph-engine · reasons: R2*

actions.edit ext/rob upserts a new version and sets a manual_edit flag (sragent/actions.py:158-160, 194-197). The version bump makes dependents stale -> engine.plan -> sync to-do 'Update now' (todos.py:324-348; applied live, update_policy auto); the flag -> 'Your edits' to-do (224-239) -> propagate_from (actions.py:310-336; sandbox, IMPACT call, proposal). engine.plan reads nothing from feedback/ (engine.py:57), so a stale node that already has a pending proposal is planned again by 'Update now', the pipeline's synthesize stage and process_pdfs; whichever the person clicks first changes what the other does (the proposal goes stale, or the engine re-revises on top). accept (feedback.py:706-721) is the only place base_versions are compared; the To-do keeps offering Accept until the click fails. GUIDE.md:289-292 describes the two paths as separate things.

Evidence:
- `sragent/actions.py:158` — upsert (new version) + _mark_dependents flag source manual_edit
- `sragent/todos.py:324` — sync to-do from engine.plan in the same build as the manual_edit to-do (224-239)
- `sragent/engine.py:57` — plan() builds from graph.stale + meta only; no proposal awareness
- `sragent/feedback.py:706` — base_versions compared only at accept

#### I58 · base_versions records only the nodes a sandbox wrote, not the inputs it read; commit re-upserts and stamps the accepted output current with inputs that moved after the proposal; put_node keeps sandbox dep_versions instead (two rules)

*not yet verified · high · graph-engine · reasons: R3*

feedback.record_bases stores live versions for changes ∪ sandbox_nodes (sragent/feedback.py:63-65), i.e. outputs; a dependency the sandbox only read is not recorded. commit upserts the sandbox data with the sandbox node's depends_on (688-690) and store.upsert recomputes dep_versions from the live store at that moment (store.py:100), so an accepted statement written from ext:A v3 is stamped current with ext:A v4 it never saw; for a new node put_node keeps the sandbox's own dep_versions (store.py:137-143). store.diff ignores dep_versions (270) and commit applies 'listed first' not in dependency order (681), so nodes can be left stale right after acceptance. GUIDE.md:460 says a proposal 'remembers the version of every output it changes' — true for outputs, silent for inputs.

Evidence:
- `sragent/feedback.py:63` — record_bases: ids are changes + sandbox_nodes only
- `sragent/feedback.py:681` — commit sorted(listed first); upsert with sandbox data (688-690)
- `sragent/store.py:100` — old['dep_versions'] = self._dep_versions(...) computed now; put_node keeps node whole (137-143)
- `GUIDE.md:460` — 'A proposal remembers the version of every output it changes'

#### I61 · store.upsert stamps a node current with ALL its inputs on any content change, so a human answer or bookkeeping write erases a pending re-assessment on a changed criterion

*not yet verified · high · graph-engine · reasons: R3*

Every changed upsert recomputes dep_versions for every dependency (sragent/store.py:99-100). Writers that touch a screen node for reasons unrelated to its inputs pass depends_on=n['depends_on']: the eligibility answer (todos.py:444), actions.edit screen (actions.py:127), _mark_retrieval (screening.py:476), recheck_eligibility (extraction.py:276), verify_existing (screening.py:416), rescreen_rule_only (569) and rob_one on a confirmed draft (rob.py:26). After any of them engine._changed_criteria finds dep_versions.protocol >= protocol.version and returns [] (engine.py:39-41), so the 'reassess' item and the reassess flag (screening.py:301-304) never happen; the same record is re-assessed or not depending on which button was pressed first.

Evidence:
- `sragent/store.py:100` — dep_versions recomputed on every changed upsert
- `sragent/engine.py:39` — _changed_criteria returns [] when rec >= protocol.version
- `sragent/todos.py:444` — eligibility answer upserts with the old edges
- `sragent/stages/screening.py:569` — rescreen_rule_only upserts rederived decisions, marking them current with the new criteria

#### I71 · Pinned semantics differ by node type and entry point: the engine keeps or re-extracts in place, feedback.apply_revised ignores pins and erases human marks while leaving a stale human_decision key, the verifier never sees marks; GUIDE promises pins are 'proposed, never applied over'

*not yet verified · high · graph-engine · reasons: R3, R5*

engine.plan sends a pinned extraction to 'extract' (re-run in place, edited fields kept by code, sragent/engine.py:98) and a pinned rob/synthesis to 'keep' (104-110), but emits 'revise' for a pinned synthesis citing an excluded study (156-160); execute turns pinned items into keep, not proposals (224-237). feedback.editable projects nodes to value/evidence, criteria/decision, domains/overall, text/cites/scope (feedback.py:79-93), dropping human_decision/edited/human_confirmed/notes, and _revise_target/_revise_dependent/_include_study have no human-mark guard (grep: no human_decision/human_confirmed/edited in feedback.py). apply_revised rebuilds extraction fields from the revised view without keep_human_edits (147-148, edited marks gone) and for a screen node keeps the stale human_decision key while overwriting decision (165-169); commit never checks pins (681-690). GUIDE.md:249 says a pinned update is 'proposed, never applied over it' and 'a recomputation that returns the same content is not a new version' (contradicted by I63 for confirmed RoB).

Evidence:
- `sragent/engine.py:98` — pinned extraction -> extract in place; rob/synthesis -> keep (104-110); revise for cites-excluded (156-160)
- `sragent/feedback.py:79` — editable(): human marks stripped; grep: no human_decision/human_confirmed/edited anywhere in feedback.py
- `sragent/feedback.py:147` — apply_revised extraction: fields = postprocess(revised['fields']); screen keeps human_decision key, overwrites decision (165-169)
- `GUIDE.md:249` — 'pinned: an update is proposed, never applied over it'

#### I10 · Protocol revise, search suggest/refine and test_model call the LLM synchronously in the HTTP thread; the first two hold App.lock so every poll and answer blocks, and none is a job

*confirmed · medium · ui-backend · reasons: R8*

App.protocol_change (server.py:624-653) and App.search_strategy (655-702) hold App.lock (an RLock) for the whole request and make the model call inside it (revise_protocol at 640-641; suggest_queries at 672-673 and refine_query at 679-680, each also doing PubMed count() calls), in the HTTP handler thread. Because ctx() (94), state() (851), answer() (490), mutate() (541), job_view() (471), jobs() (456) and stop() (424) take the same lock, every /api/state poll (every 3 s, index.html:2166) and every decision on that run file blocks until the call returns. These calls are not jobs: no jobs.jsonl record, no ui_jobs log (only a run.log line from ctx.log), no estimate, no Stop (stop() blocks on the lock and then has no pid), not serialized by App.slot, and refused rather than queued while a job runs (631, 662); their cost is written to run_dir/usage.jsonl by the UI Ctx's CostTracker (llm.py:499) under tags plan_revise/search_variants/search_refine. Their cost cannot be attributed to a job's usd window, because the worker sets self.job under the same lock; only test_model (880-897), which takes no lock and makes no job check, writes usage.jsonl lines (tag ui_test_model, 890-892) that land inside a running job's spent() delta (403, 413).

Scenario: 1) Reviewer clicks 'Ask the agent' on the protocol card -> pcCall() POSTs /api/protocol {op:'revise'} (index.html:843). 2) do_POST -> App.protocol_change (server.py:624): `with self.lock:` (629) is an RLock taken for the whole method; inside it `with ctx.at('plan'): planning.revise_protocol(ctx, ins)` (640-641) -> ctx.agent.chat(..., tag='plan_revise') (planning.py:117-118), a synchronous model call in the HTTP handler thread. 3) Meanwhile the page's setInterval(refresh, 3000) (index.html:2166) issues GET /api/state -> App.state() -> self.ctx() -> `with self.lock` (94): every poll, every /api/answer (answer() 490), /api/edit|bulk (mutate() 541), /api/job (job_view() 471), /api/jobs (jobs() 456) and /api/stop (stop() 424) for that run file blocks until the model returns; ThreadingHTTPServer threads pile up and complete afterwards. 4) search_strategy (655) does the same: `with self.lock` (660), suggest -> search.suggest_queries (672-673; one model call at search.py:107 plus up to six PubMed count() HTTP calls), refine -> search.refine_query (679-680; model call at search.py:130 plus two count() calls). 5) None of these is a job: no _record/jobs.jsonl, no ui_jobs log, App.estimate has no branch for them, App.slot is not taken, stop() has no pid to kill, and while self.job is set they are refused (631, 662) rather than queued. Their cost goes to run_dir/usage.jsonl through the UI Ctx's LLMHub CostTracker (llm.py:499) under tags plan_revise / search_variants / search_refine. 6) test_model (880-897) has neither the lock nor a job check; it writes to run_dir/usage.jsonl with tag ui_test_model (890-892), so a 'Test model' click during a running job is included in that job's usd = spent()-spent0 (403, 413) and in the subprocess's cumulative budget. REFUTED SUB-CLAIM: the protocol/search costs cannot land in a job's spent() window, because the worker sets self.job under the same self.lock (437-444) that protocol_change/search_strategy hold for the whole call, and both refuse when self.job is set; only test_model's (sub-cent) cost can be misattributed. OVER-CLAIM: 'no log' - revise_protocol and suggest_queries do write a run.log line via ctx.log (planning.py:134, search.py:120-121); what is missing is a job record and a job log.

Evidence:
- `sragent/ui/server.py:629` — protocol_change: `with self.lock:` wraps the whole method including the model call at 640-641 (planning.revise_protocol -> ctx.agent.chat, planning.py:117-118)
- `sragent/ui/server.py:660` — search_strategy: `with self.lock:`; suggest 672-673 and refine 679-680 call search.suggest_queries / refine_query (ctx.agent.chat at search.py:107 and :130, plus PubMed count() calls)
- `sragent/ui/server.py:94` — ctx() takes self.lock; state() (851) calls ctx(); answer() 490, mutate() 541, job_view() 471, jobs() 456, stop() 424, submit() 325 and _worker 437 take the same RLock (41)
- `sragent/ui/static/index.html:2166` — setInterval(refresh, 3000) polls /api/state; these requests block on the lock during the call
- `sragent/ui/server.py:631` — refused, not queued, while self.job is set (same at 662); because the lock is held for the whole call the worker cannot start a job during it, so protocol/search cost never falls in a job's spent0..usd window
- `sragent/ui/server.py:890` — test_model: CostTracker(0.05, run_dir/'usage.jsonl'), tag ui_test_model, no lock and no job check -> counted in a running job's usd (spent() delta at 403/413)
- `sragent/llm.py:499` — LLMHub tracker writes every call to run_dir/usage.jsonl, so the UI Ctx's in-thread calls are priced there but have no job record

#### I17 · A To-do eligibility answer closes 'pending review' proposal flags while the proposal stays open; both decision paths make the proposal stale without warning

*confirmed · medium · human-loop · reasons: R2, R3*

todos.apply `elig:` resolves every open flag on the screen node (sragent/todos.py:446-448), including the 'Feedback <id> pending review' flag written by handle_feedback (feedback.py:254-255) and the '<label> <id> pending review' flag written by actions._finish (actions.py:248), while the change set stays `proposed` and its cs: to-do remains (todos.py:303). actions.edit for a screen node keeps flags containing 'pending review' (actions.py:129-131) but, like the to-do answer, creates a new node version (store.py:95-105), so feedback.accept later marks the proposal stale and raises StaleProposal (feedback.py:711-719), which the reviewer only sees at accept time as a refusal ('Nothing was applied; ask the agent again'). No path warns before the decision that an open proposal targets the node: engine.preview (engine.py:295-312) checks graph dependents on a cloned store, not change sets, and neither the eligibility table nor the screen edit form shows proposal state.

Scenario: Setup: study r is 'awaiting' (elig to-do present, todos.py:112-115). The reviewer asks for a re-run of screening (/api/rerun step 'screen' -> actions.rerun -> _finish at actions.py:241-253: record_bases, then store.flag('screen:r', '<label> <cs_id> pending review', source='rerun')) or sends a study comment to the agent with no active extraction (feedback_target -> 'screen:r', feedback.py:254-255 flags 'Feedback <id> pending review: ...'). The live screen node is untouched (sandbox), so the elig to-do and the cs:<id> to-do (todos.py:303-312, status 'proposed') are both listed. Path 1: the reviewer answers the elig to-do include/exclude -> todos.apply 'elig:' (434-449): st.upsert creates a new version (store.py:95-105), then `for f in n['flags']: if f['status']=='open': resolve_flag(..., 'human decided: <opt>')` (446-448) closes the pending-review flag with no source or text filter. The change set file still says 'proposed', so its cs: to-do stays. Path 2: the study-page edit (actions.edit 'screen', 111-134) also upserts a new version but keeps flags whose text contains 'pending review' (129-131). In both paths, feedback.accept (708-722) compares base_versions (record_bases, feedback.py:63-65, includes the changed node) with the live version, marks the proposal 'stale', resolves the flag 'proposal out of date' and raises StaleProposal; todos.apply logs 'refused_stale' and returns the message (547-551). Nothing warns before the decision: engine.preview (engine.py:295-312) plans dependents on a cloned store and never reads feedback/*.json; the elig table (index.html:1000-1030) and the screen edit form (1539-1545) show no proposal state (the study page lists proposals only in a separate section, 1594-1600).

Evidence:
- `sragent/todos.py:446` — elig include/exclude: every open flag on screen:<rid> resolved 'human decided: <opt>' regardless of source/text

#### I24 · Store writes bypass the answer queue while a job can be writing: _auto_rules on GET, data_items/set_stage_models/accept_config after releasing the lock, two busy predicates, CLI without any lock, and a stale in-memory config during a job

*confirmed · medium · ui-backend · reasons: R8*

data_items (server.py:557-559 vs 604-615) and set_stage_models (770-772 vs 818) check idle() under App.lock but write the run/topic file, reload and (for data_items) create a Ctx and call config_lock.accept after releasing it; submit() (314) has no busy guard, so a job queued in that gap starts a subprocess that loads the edited file from disk (cli.py:35) while reload() is skipped and the accept is never recorded, leaving a ConfigChanged job failure or a later manual accept prompt (store writes are atomic, store.py:226, so no half-written store; Library.save uses write_text, configs.py:356). /api/config_save (1114-1119) has no busy guard and reload() runs only when idle (86-90), so stage rows and estimates can disagree with the config a queued job uses. CLI commands take no inter-process lock (cli.py:29), so a terminal `sragent feedback/sync/accept` during a UI job gives a lost update. accept_config (823-836) runs entirely under the lock. _auto_rules cannot run while a job writes (guard at 109 under the same lock the worker uses); it does run before queued answers are replayed (421-424) but the end state is the same because rules skip human decisions and answers override. The two busy predicates (job-is-None vs idle()) are inconsistent but safe, since protocol_change/search_strategy hold the lock the worker needs.

Scenario: (1) data_items gap: reviewer clicks Save on the data-items card. App.data_items takes self.lock, sees idle() True, releases it (server.py:557-559). Before it reaches lib.save (604), another request POSTs /api/run: App.submit (314-324) has no busy check and appends to self.queue under the lock; within 0.5 s App._worker (383-397) pops it, sets self.job and Popens `sragent run --config ui_job_config.yaml`, whose include points at the run file; the subprocess calls load_config on disk (cli.py:35). data_items then writes topic.yaml with Library.save -> p.write_text (configs.py:356, not atomic), sets stale, calls self.reload() (608) which is skipped because idle() is now False (86-90), so self.cfg stays OLD. Ctx.create(self.cfg) + config_lock.pending(self.cfg) (610-615) compares the recorded lock values against the OLD cfg -> no diff -> no accept recorded. Outcome: the subprocess either loaded the new topic file and fails with ConfigChanged (context.py:61-62, config_lock.check) or loaded the old one and the next idle reload raises ConfigChanged in /api/state until the person accepts manually. set_stage_models has the same shape (idle() under lock at 770-772, lib.save at 818 outside). (2) Stale cfg during a job: /api/config_save (_configs_post 1114-1119) has no busy guard; while a job is queued/running the run file is rewritten on disk, Workspace.get -> app.reload() is skipped (not idle), so stage_rows()/estimate() use the old cfg while the queued job's subprocess loads the edited file. (3) CLI: cli._ctx (cli.py:29-36) -> Ctx.create with no inter-process lock (grep: only threading.Lock in store.py/llm.py/local.py); `sragent accept --id FBx` while a UI `run` job is live: both processes hold their own in-memory store, each ctx.save() is an atomic rename (store.py:226-231), so the later save silently drops the other's changes (lost update, not corruption). What does NOT hold: _auto_rules runs inside App.ctx() under self.lock with `self.job is not None` (109), and the worker sets/clears self.job under the same lock before Popen and after proc.wait() (383-424), so rules never run while a subprocess writes the store; the rules-before-queued-answers order in the worker (421 _ctx=None, 424 _drain_answers -> ctx() -> _auto_rules) is real but rules.apply skips records with a human decision (rules.py:90-92) and todos.apply eligibility answers set human_decision and overwrite the decision (todos.py:439-445), so the end state is identical and it fires once per rule set. accept_config (823-836) does Ctx.create and config_lock.accept entirely under the lock, so 'likewise' is wrong. The two busy predicates (job-is-None at 630/661 vs idle() at 558/770/827) differ only on a non-empty queue, and protocol_change/search_strategy hold self.lock for their whole body, which the worker also needs to start a job, so they cannot overlap a job.

Evidence:
- `sragent/ui/server.py:557` — data_items: idle() checked under lock, lock released; lib.save at 604, reload() at 608 (skipped when not idle), Ctx.create/accept at 610-615 outside the lock
- `sragent/ui/server.py:314` — submit() has no busy guard: queue.append under lock; worker (383-397) starts the subprocess within 0.5 s
- `sragent/ui/server.py:770` — set_stage_models: idle() under lock, lib.save at 818 outside it
- `sragent/ui/server.py:823` — accept_config: whole body including Ctx.create and config_lock.accept is under self.lock (refutes 'likewise')
- `sragent/ui/server.py:109` — _auto_rules guard self.job is not None, evaluated under the RLock that ctx() holds; worker sets/clears self.job under the same lock (386-389, 419-424)
- `sragent/ui/server.py:1114` — _configs_post /api/config_save -> lib.save with no busy check; ws.changed() only marks stale; reload() skipped while not idle (86-90)
- `sragent/ui/configs.py:356` — Library.save writes with p.write_text (not atomic)
- `sragent/store.py:226` — ReviewStore.save is atomic (tmp + rename); concurrent writers lose updates, never half-write

#### I32 · planning.plan re-adopts an existing protocol.yaml before ever asking the model (so --fresh is not fresh for the protocol), topic.protocol_file silently wins over edits, the file is rewritten every run, and the stage receives the protocol by value while the engine reads the store

*confirmed · medium · pipeline · reasons: R2, R5*

planning.plan loads topic.protocol_file first, else run_dir/protocol.yaml, and asks the model only when neither exists (planning.py:19-38). After --fresh the stored node is None, so the human-edit provenance branch (27-32) is skipped and the old, possibly hand-edited, protocol is upserted as actor 'agent' with reason 'planned' (51-54): --fresh is not fresh for the protocol and launders a hand edit into an agent draft. When topic.protocol_file is set, criteria edited in the UI (edit_criteria -> apply_protocol, which writes protocol.yaml and the node) are silently replaced on the next run (19-22, 47-48, 52-54) with no provenance of the reversal and without resetting protocol_approved. protocol.yaml is rewritten every run (47-48) and key concepts re-validated (41-46, through the HTTP disk cache). pipeline.run does pass state['protocol'] by value (pipeline.py:19, 25, 37, 43) while engine.plan/execute and sync_awaiting_flags read the store node (engine.py:61, 218; screening.py:525), but plan() keeps the two equal (51-54) and the UI refuses or queues protocol changes while a job runs (server.py:629-631, 491-495, 541-545), so the original 'changed through the UI during a run' divergence does not occur; the duality is duplication, not a sync bug.

Scenario: 1. --fresh is not fresh for the protocol: `sragent run --fresh` -> Ctx.create builds an empty store (context.py:59) -> pipeline._plan -> planning.plan: no topic.protocol_file, run_dir/protocol.yaml exists, so lines 23-25 load it (possibly hand-edited) and log 'using existing'; line 26 reads stored=None, so the provenance.record of a human edit (27-32) is skipped; the model branch (33-38) is never reached; lines 51-54 upsert it with default actor 'agent' (store.py:78) and reason 'planned'. A hand edit is thereby adopted as the agent's draft with no provenance entry. 2. topic.protocol_file wins over UI edits: the reviewer edits criteria on the To-do card -> /api/protocol -> planning.edit_criteria -> apply_protocol writes protocol.yaml + the store node and records provenance (planning.py:100-106). Next run, plan() takes the `given` branch (19-22) unconditionally, overwrites protocol.yaml (47-48) and upserts the old protocol as 'protocol updated' (52-54), without a provenance record of the reversal and without popping protocol_approved (109 is only in apply_protocol). 3. protocol.yaml is rewritten every run (47-48) and key concepts re-validated (41-46; terms.validate goes through sources/http.py's disk cache, lines 29-35, so network only on a miss). 4. By-value vs store: pipeline.run passes state['protocol'] (the dict plan() returned) to search/screen_all/fulltext_review/snowball (pipeline.py:19, 25, 37, 43) while engine.plan/execute and sync_awaiting_flags read store['protocol'].data (engine.py:61, 218; screening.py:525). But plan() upserts that same dict whenever it differs from the node (planning.py:51-54), so inside one run both hold equal content, and no stage code changes the protocol node mid-run. The claimed consequence ('a protocol changed through the UI during a run is not what the running stage uses') cannot occur: App.protocol_change refuses while a job runs (server.py:629-631), To-do answers and store mutations are queued until the job ends (server.py:491-495, 541-545), and the job is a subprocess (server.py:351) with its own in-memory store that is what gets saved. So the duality is a code-level duplication, not a runtime divergence.

Evidence:
- `sragent/stages/planning.py:19` — protocol_file wins; elif pfile.exists() reloads before the model branch at 33-38
- `sragent/stages/planning.py:26` — stored is None after --fresh: human-edit provenance (27-32) skipped
- `sragent/stages/planning.py:47` — protocol.yaml rewritten unconditionally; upsert reason 'planned' with default actor 'agent' (store.py:78) when old is None (51-54)
- `sragent/context.py:59` — fresh discards only the in-memory load of store.json
- `sragent/pipeline.py:25` — screen_all(ctx, state['protocol']) by value; also 19, 37, 43
- `sragent/engine.py:61` — engine.plan reads store['protocol'].data (also 218; screening.py:525)
- `sragent/stages/planning.py:52` — plan() upserts the returned dict into the store when it differs, so file value and node are equal within the run
- `sragent/ui/server.py:630` — protocol_change refused while a job runs: 'A job is running; change the protocol when it has finished.'

#### I33 · `--fresh` over an existing folder skips graph.sync_settings, so outputs written in that run never become stale when a setting changes later

*not yet verified · medium · graph-engine · reasons: R2*

Ctx.create calls graph.sync_settings only `if not fresh or not sp.exists()` (sragent/context.py:66). A fresh run in a folder with a store.json therefore has no cfg:*/field:*/dom:* nodes while plan/search/extract/rob write outputs that list them in depends_on. upsert stores every listed edge but records dep_versions only for deps that exist (store.py:121), and stale_reasons reports a version bump only for deps present in dep_versions (graph.py:125); engine.plan's 'new field' branch does not fire because the edge is already in depends_on (engine.py:124). After the next normal load creates the nodes at v1 and an edit bumps them, nothing is reported as stale.

Evidence:
- `sragent/context.py:66` — if not fresh or not sp.exists(): graph.sync_settings(store, cfg)
- `sragent/store.py:121` — _dep_versions: only deps present in self.nodes
- `sragent/graph.py:125` — elif dep in rec and d['version'] > rec[dep]
- `sragent/engine.py:124` — new = want - set(n['depends_on']): edge present, no 'new item' action

#### I34 · Budget semantics differ between CLI and UI: the tracker's cumulative usage.jsonl cap vs the UI's per-job spent()+extra rewrite; three 'spent' totals; eval ignores the UI budget; BudgetExceeded is a traceback not a clean stop

*not yet verified · medium · models-config · reasons: R6, R8*

CostTracker sums every usd line of run_dir/usage.jsonl at construction and raises BudgetExceeded when spent >= budget_usd (sragent/llm.py:72-83), so a CLI re-run (or --fresh) whose history already used the cap fails at the first call. _write_job_config replaces budget_usd with spent()+extra_budget (default 2.0) for every UI job (server.py:344-345), so the run file's 'hard cap' never applies to UI jobs; App.spent() also sums eval/runs/*/usage.jsonl (300-311) which the tracker does not; cost.json holds only the last process's summary; and eval_context resets budget_usd to eval.budget_usd (run_all.py:82) so the UI's extra_budget has no effect on eval jobs. GUIDE.md:717, configs/README.md:132 and index.html:1253 say the run 'stops cleanly' at the budget, but cli.main catches only ConfigChanged (cli.py:413-421), llm.py:284 re-raises, and the worker marks a non-zero rc 'failed' (server.py:412).

Evidence:
- `sragent/llm.py:72` — CostTracker reads usage.jsonl at construction; check() at 83
- `sragent/ui/server.py:344` — over['budget_usd'] = round(self.spent() + extra, 4); spent() sums eval runs too (300-311)
- `sragent/eval/run_all.py:82` — eval_context resets cfg['budget_usd'] = eval.budget_usd
- `sragent/cli.py:413` — only ConfigChanged caught; BudgetExceeded -> traceback
- `sragent/ui/static/index.html:1253` — 'The job stops cleanly when it is reached'

write_needed_pdfs is called by fulltext_review, pipeline._waiting_for_pdfs, sync.process_pdfs, `sragent pdfs --list` and GET /needed_pdfs.csv (sragent/stages/screening.py:511; pipeline.py:126; sync.py:53; cli.py:93; server.py:1107-1109, a side effect on a GET), but not by the To-do answers that change pdf_wanted/pdf_unavailable, so the downloaded CSV disagrees with the To-do list. #### I67 · The manual_edit flag is the state machine for 'dependents need propagation' and flags are resolved when the job is sent, not when it succeeds; a rejected proposal leaves the graph stale so 'Update now' applies the rejected revision anyway

*confirmed · medium · human-loop · reasons: R2*

The open manual_edit flag (with from_version and dependents, actions.py:93-97; an existing flag's text is rewritten in place at 89-92) is the only record that dependents need propagation, and the 'Your edits' to-do is built from it (todos.py:224-239). propagate_from resolves it 'sent to the agent' inside the propagate job after the sandbox revision has been computed and before the person sees the proposal (actions.py:328-334); because the resolution is saved only in _finish, a propagate job that fails before then leaves the flag open. flag:fix and disagree:insist (todos.py:471-478, 508-513) instead resolve their flags in the UI process and save before the job is even queued, so a job cancelled by App.stop/cancel or a failed subprocess (server.py:409, 426-449) leaves no to-do and nothing re-opens the flag. feedback.reject (feedback.py:728-740) only sets the change set to rejected_by_user and resolves target flags naming the cs id; the dependents remain stale by version (graph.py:112-127), so engine.plan (99-112) schedules 'assess'/'revise' for them and, under the default update_policy auto (config.py:119), the next 'Update now' (sync.sync_dependents), the pipeline's synthesis stage (pipeline.py:66-68) or PDF processing (sync.py:52) applies the revision the reviewer just rejected directly on the live store. The sync to-do (todos.py:329-349) offers exactly this work; the rejection is recorded only in feedback/<id>.json, which the engine never reads.

Scenario: (1) Reviewer edits ext:R1.sample_size on the study page -> /api/edit -> actions.edit kind 'ext': st.upsert creates v2 (actions.py:157-159), then _mark_dependents(st, 'ext:R1', v0=1, ...) (160): _dependents returns [rob:R1, syn:S3] (74-82); if an open manual_edit flag already exists its text is rewritten in place (89-92), else a new flag with from_version=1 and dependents is created (93-97). rob:R1 and syn:S3 recorded dep_versions['ext:R1']=1, so graph.stale_reasons now lists them (graph.py:112-127). todos.build turns the flag into the 'edited:ext:R1:F7' to-do (todos.py:224-239). (2) Reviewer answers 'Yes, propose updates' -> todos.apply branch 'edited:' returns job {action:'propagate', from_version:1} without touching the flag (todos.py:480-492); the server runs 'sragent propagate' as a subprocess (server.py:371-375). In that process propagate_from runs feedback._propagate on a sandbox (actions.py:328-331) and only then resolves the manual_edit flag 'sent to the agent (UP..)' (332-334) and saves in _finish (251-252), which also flags ext:R1 'Update after your edit UP.. pending review' (248, source 'rerun'). So the flag is resolved in the same job that produces the proposal, before the person sees it; if the subprocess dies before _finish nothing is persisted and the flag stays open - a failed propagate job does not lose the request. (3) By contrast flag:fix (todos.py:471-478) and disagree:insist (508-513) call st.resolve_flag in the UI process, ctx.save() (565) and return the job afterwards; App.stop clears the queue with status 'cancelled' (server.py:426-437), App.cancel removes a queued job (439-449), and a failed subprocess ends with status 'failed' (409); nothing re-opens the flag, and the to-do built from it (todos.py:194-222, 241-256) disappears; the request survives only as a human_decisions.jsonl line and a job-history entry. (4) Reviewer rejects UP.. -> todos.apply 'cs:' reject -> feedback.reject sets status rejected_by_user and resolves only flags on cs['target'] whose text contains the cs id (feedback.py:728-740): the 'pending review' flag goes; the manual_edit flag was already resolved. rob:R1 and syn:S3 still have dep_versions['ext:R1']=1 < 2, so graph.stale lists them; sync.pending_changes -> engine.plan: rob:R1 is not pinned (no human_confirmed) -> action 'assess' (engine.py:99-107), syn:S3 not edited -> 'revise' (108-112). todos.build emits the 'sync' to-do 'Some outputs are out of date after your decisions or a protocol change' with 'Update now' (todos.py:329-349). (5) 'Update now' -> job 'sragent sync' -> sync.sync_dependents -> engine.execute with the default update_policy {screen,extraction,rob,synthesis: auto} (config.py:119): rob:R1 is re-assessed and syn:S3 revised on the live store (engine.py:219-231, 251-258) with no proposal. The same happens on the next pipeline run with an existing synthesis (pipeline.py:66-68) and after PDF processing (sync.process_pdfs -> sync_dependents, sync.py:52). engine.plan reads only graph.stale and graph.human_pinned; the rejected change set in feedback/UP...json is consulted nowhere.

Evidence:
- `sragent/actions.py:85` — _mark_dependents: existing open manual_edit flag text edited in place (89-92); new flag gets from_version and dependents (93-97)
- `sragent/actions.py:328` — propagate_from: _propagate on the sandbox (328-331) then resolve_flag 'sent to the agent' (332-334) then _finish (248 rerun-source 'pending review' flag, 251-252 save)
- `sragent/todos.py:476` — flag:fix resolves the flag before returning the job; 511 insist likewise; ctx.save() at 565 before the server submits the job
- `sragent/ui/server.py:426` — stop(): queued jobs cancelled; cancel(): removes a queued job; _worker 409: status 'failed'/'stopped'; nothing re-opens a resolved flag
- `sragent/feedback.py:728` — reject: status rejected_by_user, resolves only target flags containing cs id; no change to dependents' dep_versions
- `sragent/graph.py:112` — stale_reasons: dep version > recorded version -> stale; human_pinned (104-109) only looks at HUMAN_MARKS/edited fields
- `sragent/engine.py:99` — plan: rob not pinned -> 'assess'; synthesis not pinned -> 'revise' (108-112); execute applies under update_policy auto (219-231, 251-258)
- `sragent/config.py:119` — default update_policy: all 'auto'

#### I39 · The Overview's 'Run to here' on the Screening row runs up to fulltext (full-text retrieval and screening), while the Run page's '3. Title/abstract screening' stops at screen

*confirmed, cosmetic · low · ui-frontend · reasons: R1*

The Overview's 'Run to here' on the Screening row presets the Run page to until='fulltext' (steps.py:40-44 -> presetRun at index.html:714), the same stage as the Full texts row (steps.py:45-47), whereas the Run page's select labels screen as '3. Title/abstract screening' and fulltext as '4. Full texts + full-text screening' (452-453, 1238); next_action uses the same until 'fulltext' for 'Run search and screening' (todos.py:663-666). presetRun does not submit: the Run page shows the preselected stage 4 and its estimate before the reviewer presses Run, and the Overview row is labelled '3. Screening' with a done-test that itself depends on the full-text stage. The gap is between the Overview's 8-step model and the pipeline's 9-stage model, visible before any run.

Scenario: steps.py:40-44 defines the 'screen' row with run_until 'fulltext'; the 'fulltext' row (45-47) also has run_until 'fulltext', so two Overview rows run to the same stage. Clicking 'Run to here' on the Screening row (index.html:700) calls presetRun('fulltext') (714), which only sets runOpts.action='run', runOpts.until='fulltext' and opens the Run page; it does not submit. renderRun (1238) then shows the 'Up to stage' select with '4. Full texts + full-text screening' selected (STAGES at 452-453 lists screen as '3. Title/abstract screening' and fulltext as '4. ...') and the estimate() (1307) lists the stages that would run, so the reviewer sees the actual target before pressing Run. If they press Run, startRun posts until='fulltext', server _cmd (353) runs `sragent run --until fulltext`, and pipeline.run (pipeline.py:136) takes STAGES[:index('fulltext')+1], i.e. inclusive, so _fulltext (35-37) runs screening.fulltext_review (retrieval + full-text screening). next_action (todos.py:663-666) likewise says 'Run search and screening.' with until 'fulltext'. The Overview's screen row is internally consistent with this: its 'done' test (steps.py:40) requires that no record is still at title_abstract with a non-exclude decision, i.e. the row is 'Screening' as a whole (its label in todos.py:27 is '3. Screening', not 'Title/abstract screening'), so the mismatch is between the Overview's 8-step model and the Run page/pipeline's 9-stage model rather than a wrong wiring. Because the selected stage and the estimate are visible before anything runs, the effect is a label/model inconsistency, not a hidden extra run.

Evidence:
- `sragent/ui/steps.py:40` — 'screen' row: done test depends on full-text stage; run_until 'fulltext'
- `sragent/ui/steps.py:45` — 'fulltext' row also run_until 'fulltext' (two rows, one stage)
- `sragent/ui/static/index.html:714` — presetRun only presets runOpts and opens the Run page; it does not submit
- `sragent/ui/static/index.html:452` — STAGES: screen = '3. Title/abstract screening', fulltext = '4. Full texts + full-text screening'; select at 1238 shows the preset
- `sragent/pipeline.py:136` — order = STAGES[:index(until)+1]: --until is inclusive; _fulltext (35-37) runs fulltext_review
- `sragent/todos.py:27` — STEPS: ('screen', '3. Screening') - the Overview row is 'Screening', not title/abstract only
- `sragent/todos.py:665` — next_action 'Run search and screening.' until 'fulltext'

### State the graph cannot see (13)

#### I53 · meta.synthesis_considered decides which studies the synthesis has seen; every engine run (including scoped sandbox runs) overwrites it with ALL included studies and accept copies it live; pending_changes computes 'new for synthesis' differently

*not yet verified · high · graph-engine · reasons: R2*

engine._synthesis sets st.meta['synthesis_considered'] = sorted(inc) unconditionally (sragent/engine.py:432) even when plan() ran with roots and skipped integrate items outside scope (137-138); feedback.sandbox_delta captures every differing meta key (feedback.py:75) and commit copies sandbox_meta wholesale (695-696), so accepting an unrelated proposal marks a never-integrated study as considered and engine.plan (135, 149-151) never emits its integrate item. sync.pending_changes derives the to-do line with a different fallback (cited ∪ extracted when the key is missing, sync.py:65-69) while engine.plan treats a missing key as empty. graph.stale never looks at meta, so a missing integration is not staleness.
